Web Tension Lines

New guides from modern-web-guidance, broken into problem lines: who acts, what they do, what blocks it, and whether that blocker receded afterwards. For ideas, read the Unlock box on the New capability lines. 379 lines (119 new capabilities), up to upstream v1.0.0. repo

Type
Outcome
Group by
One h1 per page so screen readers can find the main heading CorrectionWinN/A Accessibility coding guidelines · Semantic structure misrepresents the page
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Use a single <h1> for the page's main heading and <h2>–<h6> for the rest (a dialog's main heading is the exception)
Blocker
With several <h1> elements on a page, screen reader users can't tell which one is the main heading (quote: "Having more than one <h1> element on a page makes it difficult for screen reader users to identify the actual main heading.")
Outcome
Win — Keeping one <h1> removes the ambiguity; this is a pure markup rule and the guide gives no support data (official claim)

Screen reader users often jump to the h1 or scan the heading list to orient themselves, and multiple h1s break that entry point. The guide exempts h1s inside <dialog> / role="dialog", because a dialog is a separate window.

Support N/A (the guide has no browser support data at all)

mwg:accessibility · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

When a heading must toggle content, don't put it inside summary CorrectionWinN/A Accessibility coding guidelines · Inconsistent roles of native elements
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
When a heading also acts as a disclosure trigger, use an ARIA accordion/disclosure where the heading wraps a button, not <details>/<summary>
Blocker
Some browsers expose <summary> as a button and strip all roles from its children, so the heading inside disappears (quote: "Some browsers still expose this element as a button, which removes all roles from its children including headings.")
Outcome
Win — With the heading wrapping the button, the heading stays in the accessibility tree (official claim)

Same opponent as in the accordion and navbar guides: the summary role is implemented inconsistently. This general guide doesn't name a screen reader, only "some browsers".

Support N/A (the guide has no browser support data at all)

mwg:accessibility:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Use list markup only for real lists CorrectionWinN/A Accessibility coding guidelines · Over-announcing
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Use <ul>/<ol> only for repeated, contiguous items; don't wrap paragraphs in a list
Blocker
Marking separate paragraphs as a list makes screen readers announce list information, so users hear confusing and redundant content (quote: "screen readers will announce list information when they encounter lists, therefore leading to a screen reader hearing potentially confusing and redundant information about the related content.")
Outcome
Win — Content that isn't a list is no longer read out as one (official claim)

List markup makes a screen reader say "list, N items" first, so misused lists become noise. The same section notes that checkboxes and radio buttons already announce their count, so wrapping them in a list duplicates it.

Support N/A (the guide has no browser support data at all)

mwg:accessibility:3 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Sync aria-invalid with :user-invalid so errors aren't announced before input CorrectionWinWidely Accessible error announcement · Announcements mistimed or unreliable
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Show errors with :user-invalid, and use a small script on blur/change/input/invalid to check it and set aria-invalid to match; until then, set aria-invalid="false" explicitly
Blocker
With :invalid alone, a screen reader says "Invalid entry" as soon as the user tabs into an empty required field, before any interaction (quote: "screen readers might announce \"Invalid entry\" the moment a user tabs into a required field that is currently empty.")
Outcome
Win — :user-invalid is Widely available, and the programmatic state now appears at the same moment as the visual state (official claim)

:user-invalid only affects the visuals; it doesn't touch ARIA. The guide also points out that Firefox and Safari expose an empty required field as invalid from page load, so aria-invalid can't be left unset and must be false. You still write the bridging script yourself, because there is no "UserInvalidChanged" event.

Support :user-valid / :user-invalid: Widely (2023-11-02)

mwg:accessible-error-announcement · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Reference the error from aria-describedby only while it's shown CorrectionWinWidely Accessible error announcement · Announcements mistimed or unreliable
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Point the field at its error message with data-error, and let the script add that id to aria-describedby only while the field is invalid
Blocker
Referencing the error from aria-describedby in the markup means it is read even while hidden, so users hear the error before interacting (quote: "text that aria-describedby references is announced even when it's hidden, so the error would be read out before the user has interacted.")
Outcome
Win — The error message is referenced only while it is visible (official claim)

The error element is always in the page, just hidden with display: none, so leaving the reference in place feels harmless. But aria-describedby ignores visibility, so the reference itself has to follow the state.

Support :user-valid / :user-invalid: Widely (2023-11-02); no support data given for aria-describedby

mwg:accessible-error-announcement:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Announce errors through one shared polite live region, not a live region per error CorrectionWinWidely Accessible error announcement · Announcements mistimed or unreliable
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Put one aria-live="polite" region on the page and write "label: error" into it about 500ms after a field is left or changed; after a failed submit, move focus to the first invalid field
Blocker
Making each error message its own live region fails because it is shown rather than changed, so screen readers don't announce it reliably (quote: "a live region that is shown, rather than changed, isn't announced reliably.")
Outcome
Win — The shared region announces by changing its content, and the delay keeps it from talking over the next field (official claim)

A common pattern is putting aria-live on each error <span> and toggling its display, which announces only some of the time. The guide uses a single announcer that receives text instead. When the field is fixed, the announcer is also cleared so the old error isn't found later while reading the page.

Support :user-valid / :user-invalid: Widely (2023-11-02); no support data given for live regions

mwg:accessible-error-announcement:3 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Silence decorative glyphs with content's alt-text argument New capabilityPartialNewly Accessible CSS generated content · Over-announcing
Unlock · brainstormArrows, separators and status icons inserted with CSS can be declared silent, or given a spoken label, right in the stylesheet, without wrapping each icon in an extra aria-hidden span. An icon system can live entirely in CSS without adding screen reader noise.
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Add alt text to content after a /: content: " →" / ""; for decoration, content: url(icon.svg) / "Save"; for a meaningful icon
Blocker
Without the / syntax, decorative glyphs inserted with ::after are read out by screen readers (quote: "Fallback: render the glyph in browsers without / support (screen readers there will still announce it)")
Outcome
Partial — The alt-text syntax is Newly available (2024-07-09); older browsers need a preceding content line without / as a fallback, where the glyph is still announced and non-empty alt text is lost (official claim)

CSS-added arrows and separator dots turn into noise like "right arrow" in a screen reader. The new syntax lets CSS itself declare "don't read this" or "read it as this". The guide stresses this is harm reduction: meaningful text still belongs in the DOM.

Support Alt text for generated content: Newly (2024-07-09; Chrome 77, Edge 79, Firefox 128, Safari 17.4)

mwg:accessible-generated-content · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Don't repeat text already in the DOM as content alt text CorrectionWinNewly Accessible CSS generated content · Over-announcing
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Use the alt-text argument only when the text differs from the primary value and isn't already in the DOM
Blocker
A button labelled "Save" with / "Save" on its ::before icon is read as "Save save" (quote: "A screen reader would read it out as \"Save save\".")
Outcome
Win — Not naming the icon again means no duplicate announcement (official claim)

The new syntax invites the idea that every icon needs alt text, which then stacks on top of the button's own label. The guide also says images are presentational by default, so don't add empty alt text, and emoji alt text is only for when it differs from the official name.

Support Alt text for generated content: Newly (2024-07-09)

mwg:accessible-generated-content:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Use meta text-scale so the page follows the system font size New capabilityStalemateLimited Respecting the OS text scale · User settings get ignored
Unlock · brainstormWeb pages can follow the phone's system font size (Dynamic Type) like native apps, so older or low-vision users don't have to adjust every site. Reading and documentation pages benefit most; Chrome/Edge 146+ only for now.
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Put <meta name="text-scale" content="scale"> in <head>, size text and spacing in rem/em, and use unitless line heights
Blocker
Users set a larger system font size on iOS/Android, but mobile browsers ignore it by default and the page text stays the same (quote: "By default, mobile browsers ignore these OS-level accessibility settings to prevent legacy, fixed-pixel websites from breaking visually.")
Outcome
Stalemate — Only Chrome/Edge 146 support it; Firefox and Safari keep the default size, leaving users with manual zoom or pinch-to-zoom (official claim)

Browsers ignore the system font size to protect old fixed-px sites. The meta tag is the page declaring "my layout can take it", and only then is the system scale applied to 1rem. After opting in you must not set font-size on :root yourself, nor multiply by env(preferred-text-scale) again, or scaling is blocked or doubled.

Support <meta name="text-scale">: Limited (Chrome 146, Edge 146; not Firefox or Safari)

mwg:respect-os-text-scale · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Fluid font sizes need a rem component, not vw alone CorrectionWinWidely Respecting the OS text scale · User settings get ignored
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Write fluid font sizes like clamp(2rem, 1.25rem + 2vw, 4rem) so they have both a font-relative and a viewport-relative part
Blocker
With font-size: 5vw alone, text doesn't fully respond to the user's text scaling (quote: "The font size does not respond fully to user text scaling")
Outcome
Win — With a rem component, both the system font size and browser zoom affect the text size (official claim)

Pure vw sizes follow only the viewport width, so when the user enlarges text the headings don't move. The guide adds rules of thumb: keep the maximum no more than 2.5 times the minimum, and test 200% text resize and reflow at 320 CSS px wide.

Support — (the guide gives support data only for meta text-scale, not for clamp()/rem)

mwg:respect-os-text-scale:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

With text-scale on, don't hard-code font-size on html CorrectionStalemateLimited Respecting the OS text scale · User settings get ignored
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Leave the root font size to the browser: no font-size on :root/html, whether absolute, relative or calculated
Blocker
Writing font-size: 16px on html can stop the browser from applying text scaling (quote: "This can prevent the browser from applying text scaling")
Outcome
Stalemate — Leaving the root size to the browser lets the system scale apply, but that only matters in Chrome/Edge 146+, which support meta text-scale (official claim)

Many resets and design systems pin 16px on html as a baseline, and that one line cancels the opt-in. The guide lists it as a DO NOT, and separately warns against combining it with env(preferred-text-scale), which scales twice.

Support <meta name="text-scale">: Limited (Chrome 146, Edge 146)

mwg:respect-os-text-scale:3 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Detect text language in the browser with LanguageDetector New capabilityStalemateLimited Language detection · Must send to the cloud or load a big library
Unlock · brainstormPasted text can have its language detected on-device and get the right lang automatically on comments or note paragraphs, so screen readers pronounce it correctly, without sending the content to a server. Chrome/Edge desktop only for now, with hardware requirements.
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Check with LanguageDetector.availability(), create the detector from a user gesture with LanguageDetector.create(), and get a confidence-ranked language list from detect()
Blocker
(missing) The guide only says it reduces the need for heavy external libraries or costly server calls, without saying where the old approach fails (quote: "it enhances user privacy and reduces the need for heavy external libraries or costly server-side calls.")
Outcome
Stalemate — Only Chrome 138 and Edge 148 support it; elsewhere you fall back to a server or cloud API, or disable detection (official claim)

Uses include detecting the source language before translation and setting the right lang on content. The guide stresses detection runs locally and sends nothing to Google or third parties, but never describes a concrete failure of the old way. The model download needs a user gesture, 22 GB of storage and 16 GB of RAM, and accuracy drops sharply on very short phrases.

Support Language detector: Limited (Chrome 138, Edge 148; not Firefox or Safari)

mwg:language-detection · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Run Gemini Nano in the browser with the Prompt API New capabilityStalemateLimited Language model (Prompt API) · Must send to the cloud or load a big library
Unlock · brainstormClassify, rewrite or answer questions about images inside the page without a server, for example a notes app tagging each diary entry on-device or turning screenshots into text. Data stays on the device and it works offline. Chrome 148 desktop only for now, with hardware requirements.
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Check with LanguageModel.availability(), download the model from a user gesture via LanguageModel.create(), then handle text, image and audio input with prompt()/promptStreaming()
Blocker
(missing) The guide only lists the benefits of running locally (privacy, lower server cost, offline), without saying where the old approach fails (quote: "This built-in AI approach ensures user privacy, reduces server costs, and enables offline functionality.")
Outcome
Stalemate — Only Chrome 148 supports it; Edge, Firefox and Safari fall back to a server/cloud API or a local endpoint such as Transformers.js (official claim)

The guide covers session management (clone(), initialPrompts, destroy()), multimodal input and JSON Schema structured output. The opponent appears only as the flip side of the benefits, with no observable failure. Hardware requirements are high (22 GB storage, 16 GB RAM), and the download needs a user gesture plus visible progress.

Support LanguageModel: Limited (Chrome 148; not Edge, Firefox or Safari)

mwg:language-model · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Always write model output with textContent, never innerHTML CorrectionWinWidely Language model (Prompt API) · Untrusted content parsed as HTML
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Write model responses into textContent; when limited HTML is needed, run the Sanitizer API or DOMPurify over the **complete** output
Blocker
Model output is untrusted and can contain injected markup, so setting innerHTML directly becomes XSS (quote: "Model output is untrusted and can contain injected markup. Always use textContent or a sanitizer.")
Outcome
Win — Written as plain text, it is never parsed as markup (official claim)

The usual streaming pattern is innerHTML += for each chunk. The guide says that is slow and injectable, and malicious code can be split across two chunks, so sanitize the combined output, not each chunk.

Support — (no support data given for textContent)

mwg:language-model:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Set aria-busy while streaming so screen readers read the full response, not each chunk CorrectionWinWidely Language model (Prompt API) · Over-announcing
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Make the output container a polite live region present from page load, set aria-busy="true" while streaming and false after; don't move focus during streaming
Blocker
Without aria-busy, the live region reads out every streamed chunk (quote: "so assistive technologies announce the complete response rather than each chunk")
Outcome
Win — Assistive tech waits until streaming ends and announces the complete response (official claim)

The typewriter effect is feedback for sighted users and broken fragments for screen reader users. When the output language differs from the page, the output element must also get lang so it is pronounced correctly.

Support — (no support data given for aria-busy / live regions)

mwg:language-model:3 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Run Gemini Nano in the browser with the Prompt API New capabilityStalemateLimited Prompt API · Must send to the cloud or load a big library
Unlock · brainstormSame as mwg:language-model: classify, rewrite or answer questions about images inside the page without a server; data stays on the device. Chrome 148 desktop only for now.
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Check with LanguageModel.availability(), download the model from a user gesture via LanguageModel.create(), then handle text, image and audio input with prompt()/promptStreaming()
Blocker
(missing) The guide only lists the benefits of running locally (privacy, lower server cost, offline), without saying where the old approach fails (quote: "This built-in AI approach ensures user privacy, reduces server costs, and enables offline functionality.")
Outcome
Stalemate — Only Chrome 148 supports it; Edge, Firefox and Safari fall back to a server/cloud API or a local endpoint such as Transformers.js (official claim)

This guide is word-for-word identical to the language-model guide in v1.0.0. It covers session management, multimodal input and structured output; the opponent appears only as the flip side of the benefits. Hardware requirements are high, and the download needs a user gesture plus visible progress.

Support LanguageModel: Limited (Chrome 148; not Edge, Firefox or Safari)

mwg:prompt-api · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Always write model output with textContent, never innerHTML CorrectionWinWidely Prompt API · Untrusted content parsed as HTML
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Write model responses into textContent; when limited HTML is needed, run the Sanitizer API or DOMPurify over the **complete** output
Blocker
Model output is untrusted and can contain injected markup, so setting innerHTML directly becomes XSS (quote: "Model output is untrusted and can contain injected markup. Always use textContent or a sanitizer.")

The usual streaming pattern is innerHTML += for each chunk. The guide says that is slow and injectable, and malicious code can be split across two chunks, so sanitize the combined output, not each chunk.

mwg:prompt-api:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Set aria-busy while streaming so screen readers read the full response, not each chunk CorrectionWinWidely Prompt API · Over-announcing
Who acts

Action + object
Make the output container a polite live region present from page load, set aria-busy="true" while streaming and false after; don't move focus during streaming

mwg:prompt-api:3 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Summarize on-device with Summarizer instead of sending articles to the cloud New capabilityStalemateLimited Summarizer API · Must send to the cloud or load a big library
Unlock · brainstormPrivate documents (diaries, meeting notes, email) can get a TL;DR or key points right in the page without the content leaving the device or running up an API bill, for example a "this week in three sentences" block at the top of a long weekly note. Chrome/Edge 138 desktop only for now.
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Call Summarizer.availability() and Summarizer.create() with the same options (type: key-points/tldr/teaser/headline) and summarize long text with summarize() or summarizeStreaming()
Blocker
When a remote service does the work, requests go to the cloud, the on-device privacy guarantee is gone, and server-side API costs are incurred (quote: "This completely nullifies the on-device privacy guarantees of the native Built-in AI APIs and will incur server-side API usage costs.")
Outcome
Stalemate — Only Chrome 138 and Edge 138 support it; elsewhere the fallback is exactly a remote API or a cloud-proxying polyfill, so the opponent stands untouched (official claim)

The guide says summarization runs entirely on the device and nothing goes to Google. The opponent shows up in the fallback section's warning that polyfills may forward requests to cloud models. So in Firefox and Safari you either pay and hand over the data, or disable the summarize button.

Support Summarizer: Limited (Chrome 138, Edge 138; not Firefox or Safari)

mwg:summarizer · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Translate on-device with Translator instead of sending text to the cloud New capabilityStalemateLimited Translator API · Must send to the cloud or load a big library
Unlock · brainstormChat rooms and comment sections can translate each message in place, without a third-party translation service or per-character billing; paired with language detection, users wouldn't even need to pick the source language. Chrome/Edge desktop only for now.
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Call Translator.availability() and Translator.create() with the same sourceLanguage/targetLanguage, download the language pack from a user gesture, and translate with translate() or translateStreaming()
Blocker
When a remote service does the work, the on-device privacy guarantee is gone and server-side API costs often follow (quote: "Remote processing completely nullifies the on-device privacy guarantees of the native Built-in AI APIs and will often incur server-side API usage costs.")
Outcome
Stalemate — Only Chrome 138 and Edge 148 desktop support it, not mobile, Firefox or Safari; the fallback is a cloud translation API or a polyfill, so the opponent remains (official claim)

The guide targets ephemeral content such as chat messages and user input, text not worth sending to the cloud. After the model downloads, translation needs no network. Language packs download per language pair, and availability() reports available, downloadable, downloading or unavailable; unavailable means taking the fallback.

Support Translator: Limited (Chrome 138, Edge 148; not Firefox or Safari; desktop only)

mwg:translator · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Let height: auto transition with interpolate-size New capabilityStalemateLimited Animating to intrinsic sizes · Size changes jump instead of animating
Unlock · brainstormAny block whose height follows its content (notifications, expandable notes, collapsible sidebars) can expand and collapse smoothly with one line of CSS and no JS measuring. Good for dashboard cards with lots of dynamic content; Chromium only for now.
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Set interpolate-size: allow-keywords on :root so block-size can transition from a fixed length to keywords like auto/max-content; use calc-size() when you need math on the intrinsic size
Blocker
In browsers without support, the size jumps straight to the target value with no transition (quote: "Browsers that do not support them will perform an instant jump to the target size.")
Outcome
Stalemate — interpolate-size and calc-size() are only in Chrome/Edge 129; Firefox and Safari still jump instantly, which the guide calls functional but less polished (official claim)

A "show more" section or a dismissible alert growing from 0 to its content height used to need JS measuring or max-height hacks. The new property lets the browser interpolate, but both ends can't be keywords; one must be a length or percentage. The guide also requires setting hidden after collapsing to 0 and respecting prefers-reduced-motion.

Support interpolate-size: Limited (Chrome 129, Edge 129); calc-size(): Limited (Chrome 129, Edge 129); not Firefox or Safari

mwg:animate-to-intrinsic-sizes · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Detect at-rules in CSS with @supports at-rule() New capabilityStalemateLimited Applying CSS based on at-rule support · Feature detection is unreliable
Unlock · brainstormAnimations and themes that depend on @property or custom functions can carry their own "if unsupported, use the flat version" branch in the stylesheet instead of detecting in JS and toggling a class, so component libraries can keep progressive enhancement entirely in CSS. Not in Safari yet.
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Wrap rules or declarations that only make sense when an at-rule works in @supports at-rule(@property) { … } (it can be nested inside a selector)
Blocker
(missing) The guide only says that without at-rule() you detect via properties/selectors, a registered-property trick, and JS as a last resort, without saying where those approaches fail (quote: "When at-rule() is not an option, either due to browser support or because deeper detection is needed, you can use the guidance below.")
Outcome
Stalemate — at-rule() is only in Chrome/Edge 148 and Firefox 157, not Safari; the guide advises using it only for at-rules that shipped later than it did (official claim)

Knowing whether @property or @function works used to require detours, or a JS check for CSSFunctionRule followed by adding a class. The new syntax lets CSS branch directly. It detects by **name** only, not preludes or descriptors, and shouldn't wrap the detected at-rule by itself, since unknown rules are ignored anyway.

Support at-rule(): Limited (Chrome 148, Edge 148, Firefox 157; not Safari)

mwg:atrule-support-conditionals · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Detect style query support with an always-true query CorrectionPartialNewly Applying CSS based on at-rule support · Feature detection is unreliable
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Wrap rules that should apply only when style queries work in @container style(--a: 1) or (not (style(--a: 1))) { … }
Blocker
Detecting style queries with @supports at-rule(@container style(--a: 1)) or @supports (container-type: style) never matches (quote: "IMPORTANT: Do NOT try @supports (container-type: style) to detect support for style queries, as this will not work.")
Outcome
Partial — The always-true query always matches where style queries are supported; container style queries are Newly available (2026-05-19) (official claim)

Every other container query type can be detected through a container-type value, but style queries have none, so the intuitive version fails silently. The guide's always-true query works with any property name, as long as both halves match.

Support Container style queries: Newly (2026-05-19; data from the upstream design-token-reactivity guide, not given in this one)

mwg:atrule-support-conditionals:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Add to, clamp and animate content sizes with calc-size() New capabilityStalemateLimited Calculating with intrinsic sizes · Size changes jump instead of animating
Unlock · brainstormLabels, buttons and floating panels that are "as wide as their content plus some breathing room, up to a cap" can be written in pure CSS and grow smoothly when expanded, for example a button that stretches from an icon to icon plus text on hover. Chromium only for now.
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Use calc-size(<basis>, <calculation>) on auto/min-content/fit-content (e.g. calc-size(auto, size + 2rem), calc-size(fit-content, min(size + …, …))), with a plain keyword on the line before as fallback
Blocker
Browsers can't interpolate between a length and an intrinsic sizing keyword by default, e.g. 0px to auto (quote: "By default, browsers cannot interpolate between a length (e.g., 0px) and an intrinsic keyword (e.g., auto).")
Outcome
Stalemate — calc-size() is only in Chrome/Edge 129; other browsers drop the whole declaration and the animation becomes an instant jump (official claim)

"Content width plus 2rem" or "content height but capped" used to need JS measuring. calc-size() turns intrinsic sizes into values you can compute and interpolate. The guide draws a clear line: for a plain 0 to auto animation use interpolate-size, use calc-size() only for math; it also warns that percentages inside resolve against the container, not size.

Support calc-size(): Limited (Chrome 129, Edge 129); interpolate-size: Limited (Chrome 129, Edge 129)

mwg:calculate-with-intrinsic-sizes · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Set visibility: hidden when collapsing to zero height CorrectionWinWidely Calculating with intrinsic sizes · Visually hidden but still reachable
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Set visibility: hidden on the collapsed state and visible on the open state, and include visibility in the transition so content stays visible until it finishes closing
Blocker
Collapsing only block-size to 0 hides content visually, but screen readers still read it and its links and controls stay in the tab order (quote: "Collapsing to zero size (block-size: 0 or height: 0) hides content only visually: screen readers still read it, and its links and controls stay in the tab order.")
Outcome
Win — visibility: hidden removes collapsed content from the accessibility tree and the tab order (official claim)

The height animation looks great, yet keyboard users tab into an invisible panel. Transitioning visibility too keeps both the collapse animation and the correct accessibility state.

Support — (no support data given for visibility)

mwg:calculate-with-intrinsic-sizes:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Let containers react to child state with :has() instead of JS-toggled classes CorrectionWinWidely Styling based on child state · JS patches in styling state
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Use :has() on the container with state pseudo-classes (:checked, :invalid, :focus…), e.g. .theme-card:has(.theme-toggle:checked) overriding the container's custom properties
Blocker
(missing) The guide only says JS no longer needs to toggle modifier classes like .is-active or .has-error on parents, without saying where hand-toggling fails (quote: "without needing JavaScript to toggle \"modifier\" classes (like .is-active or .has-error) on parent elements.")
Outcome
Win — :has() is Widely available; the container's style follows the child's state directly (official claim)

CSS selectors used to go only downward, so a parent reacting to a child meant event listeners adding classes. :has() makes a form group highlighting errors or a card lifting when a child link is focused pure CSS. The guide advises scoping :has() to the nearest component container, not body, to avoid wide recalculations.

Support :has(): Widely (2023-12-19)

Switch layouts with :has() based on whether a child exists CorrectionWinWidely Styling based on content · JS patches in styling state
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Use :has(img) on the component container to switch to a side-by-side layout, and :not(:has(img)) for the no-image style
Blocker
(missing) The guide only says JS or template conditionals used to inject modifier classes like .card--has-image, without saying where that fails (quote: "Historically, applying different layouts to a component based on its content required either JavaScript or conditional logic in your HTML templating language to inject modifier classes")
Outcome
Win — :has() is Widely available; the layout follows the actual DOM content (official claim)

One card component needs two layouts, with and without an image, which used to mean the HTML-generating side knew the content and added a class. :has() lets the stylesheet look at the content itself. For a fallback, the guide prefers a server-side template class, since content is known at render time.

Support :has(): Widely (2023-12-19)

mwg:content-based-styling · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Remove the replaced-element gap with display: block, not overflow: hidden CorrectionWinWidely CSS layout and responsive design · Layout unexpectedly overflows or shifts
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Give <img>, <canvas>, <svg>, <video> and <iframe> a block outer display (display: block/grid/flex) to remove the gap at its source
Blocker
These replaced elements are display: inline by default, which adds a gap in some layouts (quote: "certain replaced elements like <canvas>, <img>, <svg>, <video>, <iframe> use display: inline by default, which can add a gap in certain layouts.")
Outcome
Win — With block display the gap disappears, with no clipping needed to hide it (official claim)

When a few stray px appear under an image, the usual "fix" is overflow: hidden on the parent to clip them, which only hides the layout problem. The guide's principle is not to mask layout issues with overflow: hidden and to fix them at the source.

mwg:css-layout:3 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Exclude with :not() instead of overriding with a later rule CorrectionWinN/A CSS: modern architecture · Cascade overrides don't match intent
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Use :not() to exclude irrelevant states or targets, e.g. li:not(:last-child), button:hover:not(:disabled)
Blocker
Styling first and overriding with another rule breaks when the two rules swap order, so disabled buttons get a hover background; the override can also clobber a value set elsewhere (quote: "If we reorder the two rules, we will get a hover background on disabled buttons!")
Outcome
Win — A selector that doesn't overmatch works regardless of rule order (official claim)

"Add a border to all, then remove it from the last" is common, but it doesn't express the real intent and depends on rule order. The guide separates two cases: use :not() to exclude irrelevant targets; overriding for specialization (buttons are neutral, primary ones blue) is fine.

Support N/A (the guide has no browser support data at all)

mwg:css · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Exclude nested subtrees with @scope, not :not() plus descendants CorrectionWinN/A CSS: modern architecture · Cascade overrides don't match intent
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Use @scope (.card) to (.content) { … } to select elements inside the card but not inside .content
Blocker
.card :not(.content *) doesn't work as expected with nested cards and excludes the wrong elements (quote: "For example, .card :not(.content *) will not work as expected for nested cards.")
Outcome
Win — @scope accounts for proximity, so nested cards work (official claim)

:not() only asks whether some ancestor matches, not which one is nearest, so it goes wrong once components nest. The lower bound of @scope makes exclusion follow the nearest scope root. The same guide uses @scope to fix .dark .invert / .light .invert always resolving to one theme when nested.

mwg:css:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Don't write global resets on * CorrectionMissingN/A CSS: modern architecture · Cascade overrides don't match intent
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Apply resets to specific element types or conditions, not to *
Blocker
Resets on * can't be overridden by web components or lower-priority cascade layers without !important (quote: "they cannot be overridden by web components or lower-priority cascade layers (without !important).")
Outcome
Missing — The guide only says to target specific elements, without stating what happens to the opponent afterwards

* { box-sizing: border-box; margin: 0 } is almost a reflex, but it hits every element, including parts of web components that want to decide for themselves. The guide advocates managing priority with @layer and :where(), which a global reset works against.

mwg:css:3 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Keep higher-order tokens in CSS with container style queries New capabilityPartialNewly Reactive design tokens · Cascade overrides don't match intent
Unlock · brainstormSet one --density or --tone on any ancestor and every component below switches between compact/spacious or emphasized styles, nesting level by level. Suits data tables or admin UIs with a user-adjustable density, without passing context through a framework.
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Set a custom property such as --density: compact on a container, and have descendants apply styles with @container style(--density: compact)
Blocker
With a data-density attribute and selectors, the selectors have equal specificity, so nested density regions can't override correctly and source order always decides (quote: "A major limitation of this fallback approach is that it does not support nesting elements with the data-density attribute set, since the selector specificity is the same, order of appearance will be used to determine the styles")
Outcome
Partial — Container style queries are Newly available (2026-05-19); the guide advises against using them for core features that must work in every browser, which still need the selector version and keep its nesting limit (official claim)

Higher-order tokens like density or emphasis used to be encoded as class naming conventions or as JS framework props/context. A style query reads an ancestor's computed value, so the token can stay in the stylesheet. The guide doesn't explicitly say style queries solve nesting; that is inferred from it calling nesting the fallback's major limitation.

Support Container style queries: Newly (2026-05-19; Chrome 111, Edge 111, Firefox 151, Safari 18)

mwg:design-token-reactivity · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Let each item compute its own style with sibling-index() and sibling-count() New capabilityPartialNewly Styling siblings by count and index · JS patches in styling state
Unlock · brainstormVisualizations with a variable number of items can be laid out in pure CSS: radial menus, a fanned-out hand of cards, tag clouds colored by count. Positions and colors recompute when items are added or removed, with no JS loop.
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Use sibling-index() and sibling-count() inside calc() to compute each sibling's hue, rotation or position on a circle (optionally with sin()/cos())
Blocker
(missing) The guide only says this used to need complex :nth-child loops or JS injecting inline styles, without saying where that fails (quote: "Historically, applying unique styles to each sibling in a list required complex :nth-child loops or JavaScript to inject inline styles.")
Outcome
Partial — Newly available (2026-08-18); older browsers need JS injecting --sibling-index/--sibling-count, with the native functions overriding inside @supports (official claim)

Color spectra, fans and circular layouts all need "which one am I, and how many are there". The new functions bring both numbers into CSS, so the layout recomputes as items are added or removed. The guide requires the fallback's injected index to start at 1 to match the native functions.

mwg:dynamic-sibling-styling · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Action + object
Set container-type: inline-size on the wrapper, size text and padding inside the component with cqi/cqb, and bound them with clamp()
Blocker

The same card in the main column and in a sidebar sees the same viewport width but very different available width, so vw text looks too big in the sidebar. mwg:fluid-scaling · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Action + object
Keep the clamp() maximum no more than 2.5 times the minimum, and don't let the container get narrower when the browser zooms in
Blocker

mwg:fluid-scaling:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Animate translate/rotate/scale separately instead of rewriting the whole transform CorrectionWinWidely Individual transform properties · Cascade overrides don't match intent
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Use the separate translate, rotate and scale properties, so hover or a transition changes only one of them
Blocker
Changing one transformation in transform means re-specifying the whole chain, so a single transformation is hard to animate on its own, and a hover transform overrides a running translate animation (quote: "any changes to a single transformation require re-specifying the entire transformation chain. This makes it tricky to animate or transition a single transformation.")
Outcome
Win — Widely available; animations and transitions on different properties don't override each other, e.g. hover changes only scale while the translate float animation keeps running (official claim)

A card that floats up and down and also scales on hover makes both fight over one property when written with transform. As separate properties they run independently. The cost: the order is fixed (translate → rotate → scale → transform), so a different order still needs transform, and the two stack: scale: 2; transform: scale(3) gives 6×.

Support Individual transform properties: Widely (2022-08-05)

mwg:individual-transform-properties · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Give transformable elements an identity transform so hover doesn't change stacking CorrectionWinWidely Individual transform properties · Layout unexpectedly overflows or shifts
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
On elements that may be transformed on a state change, set translate: 0px; rotate: 0deg; scale: 1; in the base state
Blocker
Applying a transform only on hover creates a new stacking context at that moment, causing unexpected layout or z-index shifts and affecting anchor positioning (quote: "This prevents unexpected layout or z-index shifts caused by creating a new stacking context only on hover.")
Outcome
Win — The stacking context and containing block exist from the base state, so nothing changes on hover (official claim)

On mouseover an element suddenly moves above or below another, or a fixed-position child switches reference box. Transforms create a stacking context and containing block, so having them only on hover makes things jump. An identity transform makes them exist from the start.

Support Individual transform properties: Widely (2022-08-05)

mwg:individual-transform-properties:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Use overflow-clip-margin to leave a safety zone so overflow: hidden doesn't slice child shadows New capabilityStalemateLimited Controlling where overflow is clipped · Clipping cuts off what it shouldn't
Unlock · brainstormClipping and letting effects bleed used to be either/or → a card can clip overflowing text while button glows, lifted shadows and corner badges paint a little past the edge, with no extra wrapper. Firefox 148 only for now
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Set overflow: clip plus a length overflow-clip-margin (e.g. 15px) on the container, pushing the clip edge outward so a child's shadow can paint outside it
Blocker
overflow: hidden always clips at the padding box, so a child's prominent shadow is cut off at the container edge; the old workaround was an extra wrapper with padding and negative margins (quote: "Without this, the child's shadow is clipped at the parent's boundary.")
Outcome
Stalemate — overflow-clip-margin is only supported in Firefox 148; other browsers fall back to overflow: hidden and the shadow is still clipped (official claim)

A submit button with a glowing shadow sits in a card that uses overflow: hidden to clip other overflow, and the shadow gets sliced flat. overflow-clip-margin moves the clip line outward without changing layout geometry. Chrome, Edge and Safari don't support it yet, so in practice you still rely on the fallback.

Support overflow-clip-margin: Limited (Firefox 148 only); overflow: clip itself Widely (2022-09-12)

mwg:overflow-clipping-control · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
On a rounded, padded container set overflow: clip; overflow-clip-margin: content-box so the clip edge aligns with the content box and inner media and footers get concentric inner corners
Blocker
(missing) The guide only says it saves manual padding calc() and fixes "awkward nesting curves", without saying when or how the old inner corners go wrong (quote: "solving awkward nesting curves without custom calc() logic.")
Outcome
Stalemate — overflow-clip-margin is only supported in Firefox 148; other browsers fall back to overflow: hidden, clipping roughly at the padding box (official claim)

A rounded card with a full-bleed image and a dark footer needs inner corners concentric with the outer ones, which used to mean computing outer radius minus padding by hand. Aligning the clip edge to the content box lets the browser derive the curve. Support is too narrow for anything but progressive enhancement.

mwg:overflow-clipping-control:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Wrap repeated style logic in parameterized @function functions New capabilityStalemateLimited Reducing style repetition with CSS functions · Style logic duplicated everywhere
Unlock · brainstormStylesheet 'formulas' used to exist only in preprocessors at build time → a design system can ship runtime functions like --fancy-gradient(color1, color2) or --grid-template(columns) that return different values per media or container condition. Chromium only for now
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Define @function --name(--param <type>, --param: default) returning a value via result, wrapping logic like gradient tokens or column counts that switch with @media, and call it in property values
Blocker
(missing) The guide only says large stylesheets accumulate repetitive logic, without saying when the repetition causes an observable problem (quote: "Maintaining large stylesheets often leads to repetitive logic, especially when dealing with design system tokens like gradients or responsive layout patterns.")
Outcome
Stalemate — @function is only in Chrome/Edge 139; Firefox and Safari don't support it, the whole value becomes invalid there, so you need a fallback value first or a preprocessor like Sass (official claim)

The same gradient and the same 'stack when narrow, N columns when wide' rule get copied all over the stylesheet. @function turns them into one typed function with defaults, and result inside it resolves via the cascade to the last matching value. Two major browsers haven't shipped it, so cross-browser projects still lean on preprocessors.

mwg:responsive-design · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Reserve space for images and video with aspect-ratio CorrectionWinN/A Responsive design · Layout unexpectedly overflows or shifts
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Set aspect-ratio on media elements like <img> and <video> so their height is reserved before they load
Blocker
Without reserved space, media pushes content down when it loads, causing cumulative layout shift (quote: "to reserve space during loading and prevent Cumulative Layout Shift (CLS).")
Outcome
Win — The ratio fixes the size up front, so the layout doesn't move before or after assets load (official claim)

The paragraph you're reading jumps down because an image above it just finished downloading. Declaring the ratio lets the browser reserve the right space before the image arrives. This is the same point as the second line of the css-layout guide.

mwg:responsive-design:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Fluid type: mix vw and rem in clamp(), with the max no more than 2.5× the min CorrectionWinN/A Responsive design · User settings get ignored
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Write font sizes like clamp(2rem, 1rem + 5vw, 4rem) with font-relative bounds and a maximum at most 2.5× the minimum; never use vw alone for font size
Blocker
Viewport units don't grow when users zoom, so vw-only type gets too small or too large on extreme screens and doesn't grow when the page is zoomed (quote: "Viewport units don't grow when users zoom")
Outcome
Win — With the max at most 2.5× the min, browser zoom up to 500% can still scale text to 200%, meeting WCAG 1.4.4 (official claim)

A heading set in 5vw is absurdly large on ultrawide screens and unreadably small on small phones, and a low-vision user zooming in barely sees it change because vw only looks at viewport width. Mixing in rem and clamping between font-relative bounds makes text respond to zoom.

mwg:responsive-design:3 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Store combined 'OS preference or manual toggle' conditions in a custom property and write rules once with style queries New capabilityPartialNewly Combining selectors and at-rule conditions · Style logic duplicated everywhere
Unlock · brainstormCross-type conditions (media plus selector plus container) used to mean copying rules under every combination → themes, density, or 'wide screen and compact mode chosen' can be computed into one flag that every component reads, so adding a new trigger source doesn't touch component styles
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
On :root, set --color-scheme from @media (prefers-color-scheme) and from :has(meta[name="color-scheme"]) or a class, then apply each state's rules with @container style(--color-scheme: dark) and not style(...)
Blocker
(missing) The guide only says the naive approach duplicates code — the same dark rules written once under the media query and again under :root.dark — without saying when that duplication causes an observable problem (quote: "The naive approach leads to duplicated code")
Outcome
Partial — Container style queries are Newly available; for non-critical branching, put defaults outside @container and only override with style queries, at the cost of coupled values; critical branching still has to fall back to duplicated rules where unsupported (official claim)

Dark mode can come from the OS preference or from a toggle on the site, and both need the same styles, so the rules get copied twice. Computing 'is it dark right now' into one custom property first lets rules key off that property alone. The catch: the property must be set on an ancestor of everything that needs to match.

Support Container style queries: Newly (2026-05-19)

mwg:selector-atrule-combinations · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Use container queries so components look at their own space, not the whole screen CorrectionWinWidely Size-aware styling · Layout depends on the wrong measurement
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Set container-type: inline-size on a wrapper and have the component switch to a side-by-side layout or reveal extra details with @container (min-width: 400px)
Blocker
The same card or nav component may land in a narrow sidebar or a wide main column; viewport media queries require knowing in advance where it lives and writing a set for every layout (quote: "You do not need to know where the component will live or write complex media queries to handle every possible layout.")

A card is wide enough to go side-by-side in the main column, but the same card in a sidebar should stack, even though the viewport width hasn't changed. mwg:size-aware-styling · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

mwg:style-parent-with-has · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Start aria-invalid at "false", then sync it with :user-invalid CorrectionWinWidely Styling a form group when a field is invalid with :has() · Announcements mistimed or unreliable
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Set aria-invalid="false" on every field initially, and on blur, change and invalid events switch it to "true" or back depending on whether :user-invalid matches
Blocker
:user-invalid doesn't sync ARIA; left unset, Firefox and Safari expose empty required fields as invalid from page load, while Chromium doesn't expose them as invalid even after interaction unless aria-invalid="true" is set (quote: "Firefox and Safari expose an empty required field as invalid from page load, and Chromium doesn't expose an empty required field as invalid after interaction unless aria-invalid="true" is set.")
Outcome
Win — The sync script keeps what assistive tech hears consistent with the on-screen error styling (official claim)

The red border only appears after the user leaves the field, yet a screen reader may announce 'invalid' as soon as the page loads, or say nothing when there really is an error. An explicit "false" starting value plus a script that follows :user-invalid keeps both sides aligned.

mwg:style-parent-with-has:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Attach the error message to aria-describedby only while the field is invalid CorrectionWinWidely Styling a form group when a field is invalid with :has() · Announcements mistimed or unreliable
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Point the field's data-error at the error message id, and have the script add that id to aria-describedby only while the field is invalid, removing it once fixed; don't reference it in the markup
Blocker
The error element is always in the page, and text referenced by aria-describedby is announced even when hidden (quote: "text that aria-describedby references is announced even when it's hidden.")
Outcome
Win — The message is linked only while shown, so what users hear on focus matches the screen (official claim)

A user tabs into a field for the first time, hasn't typed anything, and already hears a 'required' error, because the hidden message was wired to the description in the markup. Attaching it dynamically means errors are only read when they actually appear.

mwg:style-parent-with-has:3 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Let components react to a context flag with style queries instead of descendant selectors that hard-wire both sides New capabilityPartialNewly Semantic component adaptation · Style rules hard-wired to structure
Unlock · brainstormComponents used to need every context's class name in their selectors, and couldn't be reached inside web components → a component library can ship components that 'switch to a filled button and show a badge in promo contexts', and a page only sets --surface: featured on any ancestor, even reaching components inside shadow DOM
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Set a semantic flag on the context container (--surface: featured) and let components decide via @container style(--surface: featured) whether to show a badge, switch to a filled button or change direction; don't set container-type
Blocker
Descendant selectors (.featured .button) must encode both sides, have to change whenever either side does, and can't cross shadow DOM boundaries (quote: "the selector must encode both sides of the relationship and must change whenever either side does.")
Outcome
Partial — Container style queries are Newly available; the fallback ships a :where(.featured) selector default that the style query overrides where supported, but during fallback the component stays tied to class names and DOM placement (official claim)

Adding a new kind of featured section means going back and adding a selector to every component that varies; adding a new varying component means listing every featured section. With a flag, the side that creates the context and the side that reacts never name each other and grow independently. For fixed one-off relationships, the guide still prefers descendant selectors.

Support Container style queries: Newly (2026-05-19)

mwg:usage-aware-component-variations · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Animate the native select picker with appearance: base-select and @starting-style New capabilityPartialLimited Animated select picker · Size changes jump instead of animating
Unlock · brainstormNative dropdowns used to pop open instantly, and animation meant giving up the native element → small dropdowns like theme or language pickers keep native <select> form behavior while getting a design-system open animation and rotating arrow. Firefox falls back to the system menu
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Set appearance: base-select on <select> and ::picker(select), transition the picker's opacity, height, display and overlay with transition-behavior: allow-discrete and @starting-style, and rotate the arrow via :open::picker-icon
Blocker
The native dropdown UI was rendered outside what the page controls, so it simply couldn't be animated (quote: "Previously, animating native select dropdowns was impossible because their UI was rendered outside the accessible viewport constraints.")
Outcome
Partial — Customizable <select> is Limited (Chrome/Edge 135, Safari 27, not Firefox); unsupported browsers fall back to the OS dropdown, which still works but isn't animated (official claim)

Making a menu fade and grow out of its trigger was impossible with native <select>, so people swapped in a JS library. base-select makes the picker an ordinary page element, and its top-layer enter/exit animation is plain CSS. The guide also requires turning the animation off under prefers-reduced-motion.

Support Customizable <select>: Limited (Chrome 135, Edge 135, Safari 27; not Firefox)

mwg:animated-select-picker · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Write allow-discrete as a separate transition-behavior, not in the transition shorthand CorrectionPartialLimited Animated select picker · Side effects of a new feature
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Keep only properties and timing in the transition shorthand and add a separate transition-behavior: allow-discrete declaration
Blocker
Putting allow-discrete inside the transition shorthand makes older browsers drop the entire transition declaration (quote: "it will make older browsers ignore the entire transition declaration.")
Outcome
Partial — Declared separately, browsers that don't know allow-discrete drop only that line and keep the other transitions; the customizable select the animation depends on is still Limited (official claim)

allow-discrete is needed so the picker can transition to and from display: none, but slipping it into the shorthand means older browsers skip even the opacity transition. Split into two declarations, the new syntax only breaks itself.

Support Customizable <select>: Limited; no support data for transition-behavior in this guide

mwg:animated-select-picker:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Don't set overflow: hidden on ::picker(select) CorrectionPartialLimited Animated select picker · Hiding or disabling locks people out
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Keep the picker scrollable while animating its height; don't add overflow: hidden to ::picker(select)
Blocker
At high zoom or in a short viewport not all options fit, and if the picker can't scroll the rest can't be reached (quote: "The picker must stay scrollable so that options that don't fit in the available space, for example at high zoom or in a short viewport, can still be reached.")
Outcome
Partial — With scrolling intact every option is reachable; this pitfall only exists in browsers that support base-select (Limited) (official claim)

When animating height: 0 → auto it feels natural to add overflow: hidden to keep content from spilling out, and then a user zoomed to 200% can neither see nor scroll to the later options. The picker must stay scrollable.

Support Customizable <select>: Limited

mwg:animated-select-picker:3 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Give every field an autocomplete token, using address-line1/2 when splitting addresses CorrectionWinWidely Building an address form that follows best practice · Autofill fails
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Add the right autocomplete to every <input>, <select> and <textarea>; prefer one <textarea autocomplete="street-address">, or address-line1/address-line2 tokens if the data model needs separate lines, with only the first required
Blocker
Fields without tokens aren't autofilled, so users retype data; splitting the street address across inputs without these tokens breaks autofill (quote: "Don't split the street address across inputs that lack these tokens")
Outcome
Win — autocomplete is Widely available; with the right tokens autofill works, and where it isn't supported users simply type (official claim)

Address forms are among the most autofilled forms, yet they're often split into custom 'Address 1', 'Address 2', 'Number' fields the browser doesn't recognize, so the whole block stays empty. Correct tokens, or a single multi-line field, bring the saved address in at once.

Support html.elements.input.autocomplete: Widely (2015-07-29)

mwg:autofill-address-form · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Use a single name field with autocomplete="name" where possible CorrectionWinWidely Building an address form that follows best practice · Forms assume the wrong data shape
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Unless you really need to store given name, family name or honorifics separately, use one name field with autocomplete="name"
Blocker
(missing) The guide only lists benefits of a single field (simpler, pasteable, simpler autofill), without directly saying when split fields fail (quote: "Using a single name input makes forms less complex, enables cut-and-paste, and makes autofill simpler.")
Outcome
Win — autocomplete is Widely available; a single field can be pasted into and autofilled whole (official claim)

Separate 'first' and 'last' boxes assume every name splits that way. The guide says don't split without a reason, so pasting and autofill only deal with one field.

Support html.elements.input.autocomplete: Widely (2015-07-29)

mwg:autofill-address-form:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Validate names and addresses with Unicode letter matching, not Latin-only CorrectionWinN/A Building an address form that follows best practice · Forms assume the wrong data shape
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Use Unicode letter matching (e.g. \p{L}) in validation regexes and make sure the backend handles Unicode input and output securely
Blocker
Latin-only regexes shut out users whose names or addresses contain non-Latin characters (quote: "Latin-only excludes users with names or addresses that include characters that aren't in the Latin alphabet.")
Outcome
Win — With Unicode matching, these users' input passes validation (official claim)

[A-Za-z]+ looks like a sensible name check, but names like 王小明 or Олег fail it, so the form rejects real names. A Unicode letter class lets the same rule cover every script.

Support N/A (the guide only says "Unicode in regular expressions is well supported by modern browsers." with no Baseline data)

mwg:autofill-address-form:3 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Add a non-color indicator with :autofill instead of relying on the browser tint CorrectionPartialLimited Styling autofilled form fields · Focus or state indicators disappear
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Give input:autofill at least one indicator that isn't color, such as a thicker inset ring box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 2px …
Blocker
The browser's default autofill highlight is color only (quote: "The browser's default highlight is color only.")
Outcome
Partial — :autofill is Limited (Chrome/Edge 110, Safari 15; Firefox only matches username and password fields); unsupported browsers keep the default tint and lose the non-color indicator (official claim)

Users who can't tell the tint apart can't see which fields the browser filled. An inset ring makes it visible by shape too. Firefox's other fields have no workaround, and the guide explicitly says not to add a JavaScript fallback.

Support :autofill: Limited (Chrome 110, Edge 110, Safari 15; not Firefox)

mwg:autofill-highlight-inputs · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

If autofill styling uses outline, make the focus outline differ in more than color CorrectionPartialLimited Styling autofilled form fields · Focus or state indicators disappear
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Don't remove the focus outline; if the autofill style itself uses outline, give :autofill:focus-visible an outline that differs in thickness, style or offset
Blocker
When autofill and focus both use outlines that differ only in color, you can't tell which field has focus (quote: "give :autofill:focus-visible an outline that differs in more than color (e.g. thickness, style, or offset) so focus remains perceivable.")
Outcome
Partial — The two states differ in shape, so focus stays perceivable; :autofill itself is Limited (official claim)

A whole column of fields gets autofilled with the same outline, and as a keyboard user tabs through, the focused one differs only slightly in color, which is as good as no focus ring. Making focus differ in thickness or offset keeps it distinguishable.

Support :autofill: Limited

mwg:autofill-highlight-inputs:2 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Don't change border-width or padding in autofill styles CorrectionPartialLimited Styling autofilled form fields · Layout unexpectedly overflows or shifts
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
In :autofill only change properties that don't affect layout (inset shadows, border color), not border-width or padding
Blocker
The autofill state toggles with user activity, so layout-affecting properties make content shift (quote: "the state toggles with user activity so that would shift content.")
Outcome
Partial — An inset shadow draws the ring and layout stays put when the state toggles; :autofill itself is Limited (official claim)

The user picks an autofill suggestion, borders thicken and the form pushes down; they edit a field, the state clears and it shrinks back. An inset shadow is just as visible without changing geometry.

Support :autofill: Limited

mwg:autofill-highlight-inputs:3 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Give payment fields cc-* autocomplete tokens and prefix billing address fields CorrectionWinWidely Building a payment form that follows best practice · Autofill fails
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Use autocomplete="cc-number", cc-name, cc-exp and cc-csc for card fields; default billing to the delivery address and distinguish sections with shipping/billing prefixes (e.g. billing address-line1)
Blocker
Without autocomplete, users may write card details down or store them insecurely (quote: "Without autocomplete, users may keep a physical record of payment card details or store them insecurely.")
Outcome
Win — With the right tokens the browser can securely store and autofill card and address data; where unsupported, users type and types and validation still work (official claim)

Every checkout means pulling out the card and copying sixteen digits, so some people just keep the number in a notes file. With proper tokens the browser keeps and fills it; prefixes keep shipping and billing addresses from filling into each other.

mwg:autofill-payment-form:3 · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Use username/current-password so password managers can save and fill sign-in fields CorrectionWinWidely Building a sign-in form that follows best practice · Autofill fails
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Use autocomplete="username" on the email field and autocomplete="current-password" on the password field; keep name/id stable, not random, inside a <form> with a submit button
Blocker
Without autocomplete, users are more likely to write sign-in details down or store them insecurely on their device; with randomly generated name/id values or no <form>, browsers won't store the data (quote: "Without autocomplete, users may be more likely to keep a physical record of sign-in details, or store sign-in data insecurely on their device.")
Outcome
Win — The browser recognizes field roles and can securely store and autofill credentials; where unsupported, users type them (official claim)

The password manager has the credentials, yet the sign-in page shows no suggestion, maybe because field names change with every deploy or the 'form' is just a pile of <div>s. Clear roles and stable names connect saving and filling.

Support No autocomplete support data in this guide (Widely 2015-07-29 per autofill-address-form); Email/telephone/URL input types and inputmode are Widely

mwg:autofill-sign-in-form · Upstream guide · Line file · v1.0.0

Turn off spellcheck and autocorrect on username and email fields CorrectionPartialNewly Building a sign-in form that follows best practice · Forms assume the wrong data shape
Who acts
A developer (or a coding agent)
Action + object
Add spellcheck="false" to username and email fields; also turn off autocorrect and autocapitalize on non-email usernames (type="email" already disables them)
Blocker
Usernames are identifiers, not prose, and the browser can 'correct' them into something the account doesn't match (quote: "Add spellcheck="false" so the browser does not rewrite them into something the account does not match.")
Outcome
Partial — autocorrect is Newly available (2026-09-11); with these off, the browser no longer rewrites identifiers (official claim)

Spellcheck, autocorrect and autocapitalize are designed for prose; applied to a username, the submitted string no longer matches the account. Turn them off on identifier fields and leave the defaults on free-text fields like messages and comments.

Support autocorrect: Newly (2026-09-11); no support data for spellcheck or autocapitalize in this guide

Sign-up is the moment the browser learns the credentials; if field roles are unclear, the user has to remember them for every later sign-in. Marking new-password is what makes the browser offer and save a generated strong password.
